usage: memory-fabric [-h] [--version] [--db DB] [--estimator ESTIMATOR]
                     [--json] [--share-policy {value,reviewed}]
                     [--log-format {text,json}] [--log-level LOG_LEVEL]
                     [--keys DIR] [--create]
                     {overview,tenants,anchor,checkpoint,doctor,attention,list,show,lineage,audit,episodes,search,retention,validate,release,forget,quarantine,maintain,serve,mcp,erase,erasure-scope,agent-api,offer,federate,agent-api-issue,init,incident,lifecycle,posture,receipt-verify,access-log-verify,audit-export,import,import-docs,collection,branch,merge,context,correct,suspicious,review,share,agent-api-revoke,migrate-keys,backup,restore,export-erasure-ledger,demo,sandbox,pilot-report,connect,models,tour} ...

Inspect a Memory Fabric store. Read-only unless a command says otherwise.

positional arguments:
  {overview,tenants,anchor,checkpoint,doctor,attention,list,show,lineage,audit,episodes,search,retention,validate,release,forget,quarantine,maintain,serve,mcp,erase,erasure-scope,agent-api,offer,federate,agent-api-issue,init,incident,lifecycle,posture,receipt-verify,access-log-verify,audit-export,import,import-docs,collection,branch,merge,context,correct,suspicious,review,share,agent-api-revoke,migrate-keys,backup,restore,export-erasure-ledger,demo,sandbox,pilot-report,connect,models,tour}
    overview            Store health and what it can and cannot tell you
    tenants             Which tenants this store holds
    anchor              Anchor the integrity checkpoint off the store (dir:,
                        git:, https://), or verify anchors
    checkpoint          Print an integrity checkpoint, or verify against a
                        saved one
    doctor              Whether this installation's answers can be believed
                        (exit 0/1/5)
    attention           Triage list: what needs a human, worst first
    list                List memories
    show                Why the system remembers one memory
    lineage             Derivation tree in either direction
    audit               Every logged event that touched one memory
    episodes            List episodes and their verified outcomes
    search              Search the index without logging anything
    retention           Preview retention; --apply to act on it
    validate            WRITES: promote a candidate to active
    release             WRITES: return a quarantined memory to candidate
    forget              WRITES: deprecate a memory
    quarantine          WRITES: quarantine a memory and contest its
                        descendants. Quarantine on evidence about the memory,
                        not on it having been in context at a failure - that
                        policy makes an agent much worse.
    maintain            Run the background memory manager (also started by
                        serve, mcp and agent-api)
    serve               Start the read-only web dashboard
    mcp                 WRITES: serve this store to one agent over MCP
                        (stdio). The agent can recall, fetch, verify and
                        remember; it cannot record outcomes or see withdrawn
                        memories
    erase               WRITES: crypto-erase one memory's content (provenance
                        and audit stay), then report what the erasure reached
                        and what it could not
    erasure-scope       What an erasure of one memory reached, kept, and could
                        not reach
    agent-api           WRITES: serve agents over authenticated HTTP (recall,
                        fetch, verify, remember), each request verified from a
                        signed, expiring credential
    offer               WRITES: offer one memory to a peer store over HTTP. It
                        lands there as a candidate, recorded as from this
                        store; a correction here follows it
    federate            WRITES: receive signed retractions and notices from
                        peers over HTTP, and deliver this store's to them (the
                        background manager)
    agent-api-issue     Print a signed, expiring credential for one agent
    init                WRITES: set up a store, external keys and an agent in
                        one step, then run doctor
    incident            Guided incident response: list candidates, assess one,
                        --contain it (WRITES), --record FILE a reviewable
                        record
    lifecycle           Retention rules, stale and sensitive memories, and
                        erasure and retraction progress across stores. Read-
                        only
    posture             Which security controls are on in this store, and the
                        accepted gaps
    receipt-verify      Verify a signed action receipt (exit 0 valid, 5
                        invalid); needs no store
    access-log-verify   Check the dashboard access log's hash chain (exit 0
                        intact, 5 broken)
    audit-export        Export decisions, changes, access events and deletion
                        receipts as JSONL, without memory content, ending with
                        a hash-chain checkpoint
    import              WRITES: import another store's export (JSON list or
                        JSONL: Mem0, LangChain documents, plain content) as
                        private candidates waiting for review
    import-docs         WRITES: import a compiled document (JSON: doc_id,
                        version, sections with path and text); re-importing
                        withdraws sections the new version changed or removed
    collection          Collections: named sets of memories agents can be
                        attached to. list, or WRITES: add/remove a memory,
                        attach/detach an agent, grant/revoke permissions
    branch              Memory branches: list, show, diff, or WRITES: create,
                        add, revise, withdraw (each a one-change commit),
                        close
    merge               Preview merging a branch into main; WRITES with apply
                        (needs the preview's digest and an idempotency key),
                        or revert a merge
    context             Build an agent's context bundle under a token budget
                        and record its receipt (WRITES the receipt), list
                        receipts, or explain one
    correct             Preview what withdrawing a memory would touch; with
                        --apply, WRITES it and owes the retraction to every
                        store that received it
    suspicious          Memories whose claim contradicts what the store knows:
                        a corrected value restated, a value far out of range,
                        or a contradiction of an established fact
    review              The review queue. With an id and a decision, WRITES
                        it: approve (usable by the team), limit (usable by its
                        author only), correct, or reject
    share               WRITES: widen who may read a memory (private -> team
                        -> federated), through the firewall, logged
    agent-api-revoke    WRITES: revoke a leaked credential, or all of one
                        agent's; a running agent-api on this store refuses it
                        on the next request
    migrate-keys        WRITES: move content keys out of the database into a
                        directory, verified by replay before anything is
                        removed
    backup              Consistent snapshot of the event log, verified by
                        replaying it
    restore             WRITES: restore a backup made with `backup`, after
                        checking its manifest
    export-erasure-ledger
                        Export content-free erasure ids for safe
                        reconciliation of older backups
    demo                WRITES: seed a demonstration store
    sandbox             A throwaway store with deliberately wrong memories,
                        opened in the dashboard
    pilot-report        Outcome numbers for a pilot, from the log: no content,
                        agents pseudonymised
    connect             Set up an agent tool: connect <tool> --agent <id>
                        [--write]; connect --list
    models              Which model providers are ready here, and test one:
                        models [--test provider:model]
    tour                The whole idea in sixty seconds: one fact, four
                        agents, one correction

options:
  -h, --help            show this help message and exit
  --version             show program's version number and exit
  --db DB               path to the store (default: memory.db)
  --estimator ESTIMATOR
                        value estimator for display: naive_usage (default) or
                        ips
  --json                emit JSON instead of tables
  --share-policy {value,reviewed}
                        when a memory may leave this store: value (needs value
                        evidence from verified outcomes; the default) or
                        reviewed (once validated and active). Restricted
                        memories never leave either way (default:
                        $MEMORY_FABRIC_SHARE_POLICY or value)
  --log-format {text,json}
                        diagnostic log format on stderr; json is one object
                        per line (default: $MEMORY_FABRIC_LOG_FORMAT or text)
  --log-level LOG_LEVEL
                        diagnostic log level (default:
                        $MEMORY_FABRIC_LOG_LEVEL or WARNING)
  --keys DIR            directory holding this store's content keys, when they
                        are kept outside the database (default:
                        $MEMORY_FABRIC_KEYS). Without it a store whose keys
                        are external cannot be opened at all — which is the
                        correct refusal, not a bug. `migrate-keys` moves an
                        existing store's keys out.
  --create              create the store if it does not exist (otherwise a
                        missing path is an error)

# relevance_v1 — dev split

- **Arms:** none, lexical, vector, hybrid, current, store_plain, store_vector, store_lexical, real_vector, real_hybrid, chain, lexical_tiebreak, lexical_replace, hybrid_tiebreak, hybrid_replace, store_temporal, lexical_all, store_roots, store_lineage, store_all, oracle
- **Cases:** 30 · **k:** 3
- **Deterministic** unless a `real_*` arm is present: those call an embedding model, and the rest are byte-identical across runs of one commit.

## Headroom

- Best simple baseline: `real_vector` at 0.885 ndcg_at_k
- Ceiling (`oracle`): 1.000
- **Headroom: +0.115**

A ranker's threshold is a share of that headroom, never an absolute margin (AGENTS.md principle 8a). The oracle runs through the same gates with the same `k`, so it scores below 1.0 wherever a gate refuses a memory the labels call relevant — correct behaviour, and headroom no ranker may reach (principle 8b).

  ceiling check: no arm beat oracle (principle 8b)
# relevance_v1 — dev split

- **Arms:** broken_bypasses_gates, oracle
- **Cases:** 30 · **k:** 3
- **Deterministic** unless a `real_*` arm is present: those call an embedding model, and the rest are byte-identical across runs of one commit.

## Arms

| arm | nDCG@k | P@k | R@k | MRR | critical recall | critical omission | must-not-expose | irrelevant | abstain precision | false abstain |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `broken_bypasses_gates` | 0.974 | 0.929 | 1.000 | 0.964 | 1.000 | 0.000 | **0.133** | 0.000 | 1.000 | 0.000 |
| `oracle` | 0.974 | 0.929 | 1.000 | 0.964 | 1.000 | 0.000 | **0.133** | 0.000 | 1.000 | 0.000 |

## Headroom

- Best simple baseline: `broken_bypasses_gates` at 0.974 ndcg_at_k
- Ceiling (`oracle`): 0.974
- **Headroom: +0.000**

A ranker's threshold is a share of that headroom, never an absolute margin (AGENTS.md principle 8a). The oracle runs through the same gates with the same `k`, so it scores below 1.0 wherever a gate refuses a memory the labels call relevant — correct behaviour, and headroom no ranker may reach (principle 8b).
